Cited facts

This course states facts that move — feature-gate levels, API versions, project component boundaries, measured figures. Each one below was read from a named artifact on a named date, and each carries the artifact so you can re-check it yourself rather than take it on trust.

This page is generated

Generated from docs/claims-ledger.yaml. Do not edit it by hand — regenerate with node scripts/gen-references.mjs --write. scripts/standards/cited-facts-current.mjs fails on any difference between this page and what the ledger currently produces, so a hand edit is caught rather than inherited.

How to read a grade​

Two grades appear here. verified means the fact was read from the artifact named beside it. cited means it rests on a published source that was read but not independently reproduced. Facts the project has not resolved do not appear on this page at all; they are tracked privately against the module that must re-check them before it records.

The Conditions column names the artifact a figure or a level was read from, including the environment where a measurement was taken. No number here stands without it.

M6-DD-CHECKPOINT-STORMOn M6 deep-dive run 2 (plan 06-19, the folded run), labs/m6/checkpoint-storm.sh wrote 128 MiB per write, fsync inside the timed region, through the four-path harness's own probe onto the nfs path's shared ReadWriteMany claim spike02-nfs-rwx, storm run 20261118T131428Z. Warm-up, one writer at a time: storm-writer-0 (the first write after block 1 started the NFS server) 90.77 s for 128 MiB, almost all of it the server's 90 s NFSv4 grace period (see M6-LAB-NFS-COLD-WRITE's cause; the record's 1.4 MiB/s is payload over that wait, not a write speed), storm-writer-1 128.5 and storm-writer-2 85.9 MiB/s, both after the window closed. Then three writers at once: 67.7, 68.6 and 68.8 MiB/s; then storm-writer-0 alone: 535 MiB/s, about 7.8 times the mean concurrent figure (68.37). Environment: Apple M1 Pro host, Rancher Desktop VM (kernel 6.18.36-0-virt, 5 CPUs), Kubernetes v1.37.0 on kind v0.33.0, SIZE=8, six other containers on the VM at the storm's start and end (counted by the script). Run 1 (06-18, no warm-up) is superseded and stated nowhere. Laptop-local, never disk performance.2026-11-18verifiedplanning/lab-tests/raw/m6/deep-dive/02-checkpoint-storm.txt and 03-storm-record.txt (the folded captures), planning/lab-tests/raw/m6/deep-dive/grid/measurements/checkpoint-storm-20261118T131428Z/ (storm-record.md, results.jsonl, probe.py), recorded in planning/lab-tests/m6.md § Deep-dive fold
M6-LAB-NFS-COLD-WRITEOn M6 lab run 3 (plan 06-28, the folded run), the nfs path's first write of 256 MiB onto a volume provisioned in the same invocation took 74.20 s and its second write onto the same volume 0.82 s: D-32's test (rep 1 x 10 <= rep 2) reads reproduced. Environment: Apple M1 Pro host, Rancher Desktop VM (kernel 6.18.36-0-virt, 5 CPUs), Kubernetes v1.37.0 on kind v0.33.0, SIZE=8, one foreign container sharing the VM; payload 256 MiB, two repetitions, harness run 20261120T101001Z. Run 2 (06-18) reproduced it too (82.94 s against 3.09 s; six foreign containers; harness run 20261118T122454Z), and so did run 1 (06-16; 77.41 s against 3.43 s). The lab states the folded run's figures; the lesson and the M6 deck state run 2's, labelled as run 2, with its configuration published at grid-v1.2. CAUSE (probe of 2026-11-18, quick task 06-QUICK-nfs-cold): the first time is a fixed wait, not slow writing. The nfs path's server, NFS-Ganesha 4.0.8, is configured Grace_Period = 90 and holds back every new open for 90 s after each start or restart; a first write started in that window blocks in open(2) until it closes. In one window, 16 MiB and 256 MiB first writes onto two fresh volumes took 88.09 s and 91.36 s (0.27 s and 3.19 s after open returned); a 256 MiB first write onto a fresh volume started 110.6 s after the server's start took 2.12 s; a server restart re-armed the full 90 s (88.17 s for 16 MiB). The MiB/s the harness table prints for repetition 1 is therefore payload over a waiting time, never a write speed. Laptop-local, never disk performance.2026-11-20verifiedplanning/lab-tests/raw/m6/lab/d32.txt (run 3, computed from results-20261120T101001Z.jsonl), planning/lab-tests/raw/m6/lab/grid/dataplane-storage/measurements/20261120T100727Z/harness-config.md, recorded in planning/lab-tests/m6.md § Lab re-run 3; run 2: planning/lab-tests/raw/m6/lab/superseded/2/d32.txt and planning/lab-tests/raw/m6/grid/dataplane-storage/measurements/20261118T122219Z/harness-config.md, recorded in planning/lab-tests/m6.md § Lab fold; the cause: planning/lab-tests/raw/m6/nfs-cold-probe/ (run-notes.txt, the Ganesha logs and the probe JSON lines), recorded in planning/lab-tests/m6.md § Cold first write: cause (2026-11-18)

NVIDIA-MIG-TIMESLICING-ISOLATIONNVIDIA's GPU Operator documentation describes a MIG instance as a partition that provides memory and fault isolation at the hardware layer, and states that time-slicing has no memory or fault isolation between the replicas of one GPU. The device plugin's README says the same of time-slicing: each workload can reach the GPU's memory and runs in the same fault domain as the others, so if one crashes they all do.2026-11-12verifiedNVIDIA/cloud-native-docs at d856ea69 gpu-operator/gpu-sharing.rst:25-26 (time-slicing: no memory or fault-isolation between replicas), :56-58 (MIG: memory and fault isolation at the hardware layer), :65-66; NVIDIA/k8s-device-plugin tag v0.20.1 (03296a61) README.md:367-371 (same fault-domain)
NVIDIA-MPS-ERROR-CONTAINMENTWith NVIDIA's Multi-Process Service, client processes have isolated GPU address spaces and the control daemon limits each client's memory and compute share, but error containment is limited: a fatal GPU fault from one client is reported to every client sharing that GPU, and the affected clients must exit.2026-11-12citeddocs.nvidia.com/deploy/mps when-to-use-mps.html § Memory Protection and Error Containment, and architecture.html ("a fatal fault from one client may bring down a different user's client that shares any GPU with the faulting client"), read 2026-11-12; NVIDIA/k8s-device-plugin tag v0.20.1 README.md:373-376 and :536-544 (per-client memory and compute limits)

NVIDIA-XID-48-63-79In NVIDIA's XID catalogue, XID 48 is a double-bit ECC error the GPU could not correct, XID 63 is a memory row-remapping event (dynamic page retirement on earlier GPUs), and XID 79 means the GPU has fallen off the bus: the driver found it not accessible over PCI Express. A row remap takes effect only after a GPU reset.2026-11-12citeddocs.nvidia.com/deploy/xid-errors/latest analyzing-xid-catalog.html rows XID 48 ROBUST_CHANNEL_GPU_ECC_DBE, XID 63 INFOROM_DRAM_RETIREMENT_EVENT, XID 79 ROBUST_CHANNEL_GPU_HAS_FALLEN_OFF_THE_BUS (page "Last updated on Nov 11, 2026"); docs.nvidia.com/deploy/a100-gpu-mem-error-mgmt/latest row-remapping.html ("requires a GPU reset to take effect")

SPIKE06-AGENT-SUBSTRATE-NO-GOSPIKE-06 (plan 05-47, 2026-11-17) recorded NO-GO on every arm for Agent Substrate v1.0.0, for a reason that comes before memory or architecture: it cannot be installed, and its counter demo cannot be run, from released artefacts. The facts​

KUEUE-API-V1BETA2​

Kueue v0.20.0 no longer serves kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta1. Every ClusterQueue, LocalQueue, ResourceFlavor, Cohort, Topology, AdmissionCheck and Kueue Workload manifest in the course is kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2, and the configuration object is config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2. Upstream ships hack/migrate-to-v1beta2.sh for inherited manifests.

Stated inplanning/DESIGN-SPEC.md, site/docs/reference/landscape.md, site/docs/m3-kubernetes-as-the-ai-platform/lesson.md, planning/lab-tests/06-version-reads.md, site/docs/m5-ai-aware-scheduling-and-capacity/lesson.md
Re-check whenAny Kueue minor release (the project patches monthly), or any manifest lifted from 304 or from a 2025-era tutorial entering the repository.
How it was settledRe-read 2026-11-18 at v0.20.6, the course's new pin (D-46), by plan 06-02. The serving fact is unchanged from v0.20.0: only v1beta2 is served. The older version is still listed in each CRD file with served: false, so a manifest written against it is still rejected; the listed, non-served entry is not a return of the old API. verified_on deliberately stays 2026-10-06: scripts/gen-landscape.mjs renders this entry's verified_on into the Evidence cell of M3's published Kueue row (site/docs/reference/landscape.md and the M3 lesson's landscape block), and D-54 and D-46 hold that row byte-identical as a dated read. Moving the date would re-render a published M3 table for no change of fact. The date moves when M3 is next re-published.

KUEUE-K8S-134-FLOOR​

Kueue v0.20.0 requires Kubernetes 1.34 or newer as its officially supported and tested floor, and installs from the OCI chart oci://registry.k8s.io/kueue/charts/kueue, so Helm 3 OCI support is a requirement rather than a convenience.

GIE-LLMD-SEAM​

Gateway API Inference Extension v1.6.0 moved the full Endpoint Picker, Body-Based Routing and the Latency Predictor OUT of kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api-inference-extension and into the llm-d org. GIE now hosts InferencePool, the Endpoint Picker PROTOCOL, conformance and a new Lightweight EPP only. InferenceObjective, InferenceModelRewrite and EndpointPickerConfig were removed from GIE and live in llm-d-router. The former "Inference Scheduler" is now called the llm-d Router. InferencePool.spec.endpointPickerRef became optional in v1.6.0. Observed at llm-d-router v1.0.0 (SPIKE-08): the router's Standalone Mode ran with zero CRDs on the cluster; its live configuration is EndpointPickerConfig at llm-d.ai/v1; it looks InferenceObjective up as its own API and falls back to a default when none exists; and its image is still llm-d-router-endpoint-picker, with a banner that still reads "GIE build".

Verified on2026-10-06

The GIE half was not re-read (GIE is still v1.6.2), and verified_on keeps the GIE read date because it is the date of the entry's oldest fact and is rendered on M3's lesson, which plan 05-32 may not edit.

LLMD-STACK-PIN-V100​

The serving layer (labs/serving/sim-router/) pins llm-d-router v1.0.0 (released 2026-10-16) as the image ghcr.io/llm-d/llm-d-router-endpoint-picker@sha256:024188e998f516879f68532016ca24fabca00afbdcc2af9a61b17634e197df2c and llm-d-inference-sim v1.2.0 (released 2026-11-10) as ghcr.io/llm-d/llm-d-inference-sim@sha256:9992453829c0dea8d120b8e0322bb0e4237cbb3c6d9ea3bdce060410c40c91c9. Both digests are OCI image indexes carrying linux/amd64 and linux/arm64. The bare ghcr.io/llm-d/llm-d-router repository is not the image and answers 403. At v1.0.0 the endpoint-picker configuration (kind EndpointPickerConfig) and InferenceObjective are served at llm-d.ai/v1, the legacy inference.networking.x-k8s.io pool group is removed, and the kvcache_* metric aliases are removed in favour of llm_d_epp_kv_cache_*. At llm-d-inference-sim v1.0.0 the dummy-tokenizer flag was removed, and an empty or absent --render-url replaces it. M1's published lab keeps llm-d-inference-sim v0.11.2 by its own digest (D-57). These are pin and API facts only. They say nothing about whether prefix-cache routing works on these pins, or about the profile's memory. Those are SPIKE-08's to measure.

DRA-CORE-GA-134​

Dynamic Resource Allocation core — DeviceClass, ResourceSlice, ResourceClaim and ResourceClaimTemplate — went GA in Kubernetes 1.34, API group/version resource.k8s.io/v1. It did not graduate "somewhere in the 1.34-1.35 line"; the hedge reads as uncertainty to an audience that will check.

DRA-EXTRES-GA-137​

An extended resource name on a DeviceClass — the no-rewrite migration path, gate DRAExtendedResource — is GA in Kubernetes 1.37. It was alpha from 1.34 through 1.35 and beta in 1.36. A Pod asking for nvidia.com/gpu is matched through DRA with no ResourceClaim in the manifest.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-10-06
Sourcehttps://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/pull/138488
Conditionskubernetes/website .../feature-gates/DRAExtendedResource.md frontmatter (stage: alpha, fromVersion: "1.34", toVersion: "1.35"); kubernetes/kubernetes CHANGELOG/CHANGELOG-1.37.md:398, PR #138488
Stated inplanning/DESIGN-SPEC.md, site/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lesson.md, site/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lab.md
Re-check whenKubernetes 1.38 release.
How it was settledThis was carried as an open researcher conflict and is RESOLVED (D-38). The two readings in circulation were never contradictory: the frontmatter's fromVersion/toVersion pair names the release at which the gate was INTRODUCED and the last release at which it was still alpha, while the narrative source named only one of the two. The settling rule, which generalises to every feature-gate claim in this ledger: the kubernetes/website feature-gate frontmatter is authoritative over any blog post or release-narrative prose, because that frontmatter IS the source the published feature-gate tables are generated from. Rev 4's GA claim stands; its alpha version was wrong by one release.

DRA-WORKLOAD-CLAIMS-BETA-OFF​

DRA ResourceClaims for workloads — one claim per PodGroup, gate DRAWorkloadResourceClaims — is beta in Kubernetes 1.37 AND DISABLED BY DEFAULT. The missing words in rev 4 are "default-off". With the gate off the ResourceClaim controller deliberately will not create a per-Pod claim for a PodGroup member, which is a silent-pending failure mode worth a lab.

SPIKE05-M4-SYNTHETIC-LABELS​

dra-example-driver v0.5.0 fights the Lab Truth Contract with its own output. It names its synthetic devices gpu-0, advertises model: LATEST-GPU-MODEL and an 80Gi memory capacity that is not memory on any device, and injects GPU_DEVICE_0 and GPU_DEVICE_0_SHARING_STRATEGY=TimeSlicing into the container. Every M4 terminal capture will therefore contain the word GPU and a fabricated memory figure in output the author did not write, so prose discipline alone cannot fix it — the captures must be captioned, or the DeviceClass and device names relabelled where the lab controls them.

GENERICWORKLOAD-GATE-137​

The core gang-scheduling API is scheduling.k8s.io/v1beta1 (Workload, PodGroup) in Kubernetes 1.37. But the GangScheduling and WorkloadAwarePreemption feature gates were REMOVED and collapsed into a single GenericWorkload gate, which is beta but DEFAULT-OFF. Naming the removed gates on a slide dates the course to a 1.36-era draft. Two operator facts rev 4 omits: upgrading 1.36 to 1.37 requires deleting every v1alpha2 Workload and PodGroup object first, and with the gate off the ResourceClaim controller will not create a per-Pod claim for a PodGroup member.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-10-06
Sourcehttps://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/pull/139520
ConditionsCHANGELOG/CHANGELOG-1.37.md, PR #140184 (the v1beta1 API) and PR #139520 (the gate removal and collapse); kubernetes/website .../feature-gates/GenericWorkload.md frontmatter (stage: beta, defaultValue: false)
Stated inplanning/DESIGN-SPEC.md, site/docs/reference/landscape.md, site/docs/m3-kubernetes-as-the-ai-platform/lesson.md, site/docs/m3-kubernetes-as-the-ai-platform/deep-dive.md, site/docs/m3-kubernetes-as-the-ai-platform/lab.md, site/docs/m5-ai-aware-scheduling-and-capacity/lesson.md, site/docs/m5-ai-aware-scheduling-and-capacity/deep-dive.md

WORKLOAD-KIND-COLLISION​

With the GenericWorkload gate enabled, two kinds are named Workload — scheduling.k8s.io/v1beta1 and kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2 — and kubectl get workloads silently returns nothing. M5 must teach this explicitly, because the course's own cluster profile is what produces the collision.

FieldValue

AGENT-SANDBOX-V1011-SUSPEND-MECHANISM​

At Agent Sandbox v1.0.11 the core controller names each volumeClaimTemplates claim <template name>-<sandbox name>, owns it, and mounts it in the Sandbox's Pod as a volume named after the template; the Pod is named after the Sandbox. While spec.operatingMode is Suspended it deletes the Pod it owns and leaves the claim in place. The agent_sandbox_suspend_latency_ms histogram times from the controller first observing operatingMode=Suspended to Suspended=True (PodTerminated), and agent_sandbox_resume_latency_ms from first observing operatingMode=Running after a fully suspended state to Ready, both in milliseconds; neither is recorded across controller restarts or leader changes, or for a transition that completes in the pass that starts it. The agent_sandboxes gauge carries an operating_mode label. When spec.shutdownTime passes, the controller acts on spec.shutdownPolicy, and with Delete the Sandbox and its resources are garbage-collected.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-11-17
Sourcehttps://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/agent-sandbox/blob/v1.0.11/controllers/sandbox_controller.go
Conditionskubernetes-sigs/agent-sandbox tag v1.0.11 (890de53a) controllers/sandbox_controller.go:2016-2029 (the claim volume pvcTemplate.Name + "-" + sandbox.Name, the Pod named sandbox.Name), :2220 (the claim created and owned), :1753-1767 (the owned Pod deleted while Suspended), :1145-1193 (where the two latencies are recorded, and skipped when the transition was anchored in the same pass); docs/metrics.md:20, :22 and :25 at the tag (planning/lab-tests/raw/05-addition/agent-sandbox-repin/05-docs-metrics-and-configuration-v1.0.11.txt); v1.0.11 release body, #2174 (the operating_mode label); tag v1.0.11 site/content/docs/sandbox/lifecycle/_index.md ("Sandbox Expiration": at shutdownTime the sandbox and its associated resources are garbage-collected) (06-docs-lifecycle-and-volumes-v1.0.11.txt)
Stated insite/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/lab.md, site/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/lesson.md
Re-check whenagent-sandbox-minor-bump, or any release that changes claim naming, the suspend path, or these metric names or labels. Re-read before M11 records.
How it was settledRead from the source at the tag rather than from the docs alone, because the docs describe the metrics and not the claim naming or the Pod deletion the lab's commands depend on. What a resume does NOT restore is not a claim of this entry: the lab shows it on the learner's own cluster (the marker in /dev/shm is absent after the resume), and the D-64 sentence says so.

AGENT-SUBSTRATE-V100​

Agent Substrate (agent-substrate/substrate, Apache-2.0) released v1.0.0 on 2026-11-05, its latest release when read on 2026-11-17. It maps many actors (stateful workloads such as agents) onto a smaller pool of pre-started worker Pods: Kubernetes provisions the workers, and its own control plane places actors on them. Idle actors are suspended by snapshotting their memory and filesystem, and restored into a worker when a request arrives. Every actor runs in a gVisor (runsc) or Cloud Hypervisor microVM sandbox; plain Linux containers are not supported. Its Kubernetes API group is ate.dev/v1, with three kinds, WorkerPool, SandboxConfig and CSIDriverConfig; actors and actor templates are not CRDs but objects of its own API server. Neither its README nor its architecture guide names kubernetes-sigs/agent-sandbox, and Agent Sandbox's README and its v1.0.6 to v1.0.11 release notes do not name Agent Substrate. CNCF's announcement of 2026-11-10 names Agent Substrate among new CNCF Sandbox projects and gives no acceptance date. The README's performance figures (a resume time, an activation rate, density and oversubscription multiples, an actors-per-pod demo) are the project's own claims, recorded here and never printed as course facts.

OPENSHELL-V018​

OpenShell (NVIDIA/OpenShell, Apache-2.0) released v0.1.8 on 2026-11-10, its latest release when read on 2026-11-17, after v0.1.2 to v0.1.7 at about one a week; it is pre-1.0. It runs each agent in a sandbox whose files, system calls and network connections are confined by a declared policy, checks every connection against the policy, adds credentials only to requests bound for approved endpoints, and uses formal verification to flag new access a policy change would grant before it is approved. On Kubernetes its gateway is deployed with Helm, needs a CNI that enforces NetworkPolicy, and the chart fails before creating gateway resources unless the cluster serves Agent Sandbox's agents.x-k8s.io/v1beta1 (or v1alpha1) Sandbox API; its README tells the installer to apply Agent Sandbox's sandbox.yaml first. It collects anonymous telemetry by default (OPENSHELL_TELEMETRY_ENABLED=false, or server.telemetryEnabled=false in Helm, turns it off). CNCF's announcement of 2026-11-10 names it as joining CNCF as a Sandbox project, spearheaded by NVIDIA.

SPIKE01-GVISOR-GO​

Nested runsc runs inside a kind node container on BOTH architectures — arm64 (Apple M1 Pro, host kernel 6.18.36-0-virt) and amd64 (GitHub Actions ubuntu-24.04, 6.17.0-1022-azure). The decisive evidence is the kernel identity read from INSIDE the Pod — Linux version 4.19.0-gvisor — never the Pod's phase, because a silent containerd fallback to runc is indistinguishable from outside. A capability differential is NOT proof of isolation and is not offered as one.

SPIKE02-STORAGE-SCOPE​

M6.3's four-path storage measurement is GO but SCOPE-LIMITED by three fences. It may not claim a pvc-versus-hostpath difference (3.3% write, 10.6% read; and on kind the local provisioner is a bind mount, not a CSI data path — the null result is the better lesson). It may not rank nfs against s3 on write (19.3% separation against a 57.2% threshold the runs set themselves). And it may not present absolute throughput as disk performance, because of host-side page cache the guest cannot evict.

SPIKE02-NFS-COLD-WRITE​

The headline storage finding is the COLD FIRST WRITE, not steady state: NFS wrote at 3.5 MiB/s in both runs, and 69 to 125 times faster warm. The RWX checkpoint path M7 depends on is therefore roughly two orders of magnitude slower on first write. This figure is laptop-local and must be stated with its environment. CORRECTED 2026-11-18 (quick task 06-QUICK-nfs-cold, planning/lab-tests/m6.md § Cold first write: cause): the cold figure is a fixed wait, not a write speed. The NFS-Ganesha server holds back new opens for its 90 s NFSv4 grace period after every start, and SPIKE-02 timed its first write in the same invocation that started the server, which fits the same reading: 3.5 MiB/s is best read as the payload divided by a mostly waiting time (SPIKE-02's own server log was not captured). Measured times stand; the rate reading does not.

On Kubernetes it is reached through a CSI driver from a cloud provider's managed service (for example kubernetes-sigs/aws-fsx-csi-driver, the CSI driver of Amazon FSx for Lustre) or from the storage vendor.

M6-BRIEF-GDS​

TOPOLOGY-MANAGER-SINGLE-NUMA​

The kubelet's Topology Manager aligns a Pod's resources by policy; with single-numa-node, a Pod whose resources cannot come from one NUMA node is rejected from the node with a pod admission failure. Aligning CPUs and memory needs the CPU Manager and the Memory Manager enabled with a suitable policy. The kubelet's live configuration, including topologyManagerPolicy, is read from the node's configz endpoint through the API server proxy.

M6-TIER-SPLIT-RUNS​

The tier split (both local-file-system paths above both network-protocol paths) held in 16 of 16 rows in SPIKE-02 and in 8 of 8 in plan 03-05's run of the storage layer, both on the course's build machine (Apple M1 Pro, Rancher Desktop VM). The course's two runs of M6's lab on the same machine did not repeat it: run 1 (06-16) held it on write in both repetitions but not on read (s3 above both local paths in both reads, nfs in the second); run 2 (06-18, the folded run) held it in repetition 1 on both sides and broke it in repetition 2 on both (s3 above both local paths on write and on read). The split is therefore taught as a property to check, not one to expect.

M6-LAB-NFS-COLD-WRITE​

On M6 lab run 3 (plan 06-28, the folded run), the nfs path's first write of 256 MiB onto a volume provisioned in the same invocation took 74.20 s and its second write onto the same volume 0.82 s: D-32's test (rep 1 x 10 <= rep 2) reads reproduced. Environment: Apple M1 Pro host, Rancher Desktop VM (kernel 6.18.36-0-virt, 5 CPUs), Kubernetes v1.37.0 on kind v0.33.0, SIZE=8, one foreign container sharing the VM; payload 256 MiB, two repetitions, harness run 20261120T101001Z. Run 2 (06-18) reproduced it too (82.94 s against 3.09 s; six foreign containers; harness run 20261118T122454Z), and so did run 1 (06-16; 77.41 s against 3.43 s). The lab states the folded run's figures; the lesson and the M6 deck state run 2's, labelled as run 2, with its configuration published at grid-v1.2. CAUSE (probe of 2026-11-18, quick task 06-QUICK-nfs-cold): the first time is a fixed wait, not slow writing. The nfs path's server, NFS-Ganesha 4.0.8, is configured Grace_Period = 90 and holds back every new open for 90 s after each start or restart; a first write started in that window blocks in open(2) until it closes. In one window, 16 MiB and 256 MiB first writes onto two fresh volumes took 88.09 s and 91.36 s (0.27 s and 3.19 s after open returned); a 256 MiB first write onto a fresh volume started 110.6 s after the server's start took 2.12 s; a server restart re-armed the full 90 s (88.17 s for 16 MiB). The MiB/s the harness table prints for repetition 1 is therefore payload over a waiting time, never a write speed. Laptop-local, never disk performance.

M6-DD-CHECKPOINT-STORM​

On M6 deep-dive run 2 (plan 06-19, the folded run), labs/m6/checkpoint-storm.sh wrote 128 MiB per write, fsync inside the timed region, through the four-path harness's own probe onto the nfs path's shared ReadWriteMany claim spike02-nfs-rwx, storm run 20261118T131428Z. Warm-up, one writer at a time: storm-writer-0 (the first write after block 1 started the NFS server) 90.77 s for 128 MiB, almost all of it the server's 90 s NFSv4 grace period (see M6-LAB-NFS-COLD-WRITE's cause; the record's 1.4 MiB/s is payload over that wait, not a write speed), storm-writer-1 128.5 and storm-writer-2 85.9 MiB/s, both after the window closed. Then three writers at once: 67.7, 68.6 and 68.8 MiB/s; then storm-writer-0 alone: 535 MiB/s, about 7.8 times the mean concurrent figure (68.37). Environment: Apple M1 Pro host, Rancher Desktop VM (kernel 6.18.36-0-virt, 5 CPUs), Kubernetes v1.37.0 on kind v0.33.0, SIZE=8, six other containers on the VM at the storm's start and end (counted by the script). Run 1 (06-18, no warm-up) is superseded and stated nowhere. Laptop-local, never disk performance.

SPIKE03-M93-RECUT​

M9.3's prefix-cache routing risk is RETIRED with a size fence, and the fence still stands on the 1.x pins. On v0.11.x (SPIKE-03) llm-d-inference-sim v0.11.2's ZMQ cache events drove llm-d-router v0.11.0's prefix-cache scorer in Standalone Mode, and the profile peaked at 5227 MiB against the 4500 MiB lab budget (727 MiB over); the one measurable reduction candidate measured 522 MiB WORSE at two replicas (5749 MiB). The re-cut: M9.3 is REAL on 16 GB and BRIEF on 8 GB, fenced and not reduced. On the re-pinned stack (llm-d-inference-sim v1.2.0, llm-d-router v1.0.0) SPIKE-08 reproduced the mechanism in two cold runs, with zero CRDs on the cluster: the per-endpoint scores inverted across a forced eviction for a byte-identical prompt. Its memory figures, 3536 and 3556 MiB of summed container peak, are under the budget, but they were measured on a VM that carried about 3.1 GiB of foreign containers and was not restarted. The anon term did not move against SPIKE-05 (worker 1167/1168 MiB against 1140); the fall is reclaimable page cache. That memory finding therefore did not carry over as a figure and does not unfence the lab. A conditional re-cut (REAL on 8 GB only if a restarted, otherwise idle VM also measures inside 4500 MiB) is drafted for Phase 8, and the founder-approved fence stays in force until that phase decides.

SPIKE03-PREFIX-REPLAY-NULL​

M9.3 may NOT teach prefix-cache routing through a repeated-prefix replay. On v0.11.x (SPIKE-03) the replay was measured three ways and was indistinguishable from the random control, because residency saturates and observing it spreads it. That null result did NOT carry over to the 1.x pins: in SPIKE-08's two runs the prefix-cache policy reached 92% per-prefix concentration against the random control's 44% and 58%. The prohibition stands on a different ground. In the same runs a cache-blind load-aware policy reached 79% and 77% concentration and served 64% and 75% of prompt tokens from cache, against prefix-cache's 76% and 79%, because a tied score breaks deterministically and stickiness alone earns cache hits; and the policies run in a fixed order on one fleet. So the replay cannot attribute a separation to cache awareness; the controlled asymmetry is the design that holds residency fixed. The vLLM render service remains a hard dependency of precise prefix-cache routing (router v1.0.0 rejects an estimate-backed token producer for it), so M8's dummy-model fast-tokenizer guidance does not transfer to M9.3.

ACCEL-NVIDIA-SPEC-TABLES-2026-10​

NVIDIA's own product-page specification tables, read on 2026-10-29, print these memory and bandwidth figures. L4: "GPU memory 24GB", "GPU memory bandwidth 300GB/s". L40S: "GPU Memory 48GB GDDR6 with ECC", "Memory Bandwidth 864GB/s". A100: "GPU Memory 80GB HBM2e" for both PCIe and SXM, bandwidth "1,935 GB/s" and "2,039 GB/s". H100: "GPU Memory 80GB" (SXM) and "94GB" (NVL), bandwidth "3.35TB/s" and "3.9TB/s". H200: "GPU Memory 141GB", "GPU Memory Bandwidth 4.8TB/s".

How it was settledA 1.0 project on an alpha API group. The version number and the API maturity point in different directions, which is the same asymmetry as JobSet (an alpha API in production use). It supersedes the "pre-1.0, two active lines" sentence and the Tool/MCPServer CRD names in DESIGN-SPEC M11.6; see that document's Accepted Corrections row 29. v1.0.0 also moved the group from kagent.dev to api.kagent.dev, so a 0.10-era manifest does not apply to a 1.0 install.

agentgateway v1.7.0 (2026-10-27) is the latest release. Its images and Helm charts are published at cr.agentgateway.dev (agentgateway, controller, and the agentgateway, agentgateway-crds and agentgateway-standalone charts), its CRDs are served at agentgateway.dev/v1alpha1, and its release notes list breaking changes. KAR-CONDITIONAL-NA​

Some AI Conformance requirements are conditional. Cluster autoscaling is a MUST that applies "if the platform provides a cluster autoscaler or an equivalent mechanism", and a platform for which a requirement's context does not apply may answer "Not Applicable" with a justification. The justification may not be "we don't support this feature"; it must explain why the context does not apply.

PODCERT-NO-BUILTIN-SIGNER-137​

At Kubernetes v1.37.0 PodCertificateRequest is a certificates.k8s.io/v1 type, and its signerName field documents one well-known signer, kubernetes.io/kube-apiserver-client-pod, as "currently unimplemented". Kubernetes 1.37 therefore ships no built-in signer that issues pod certificates. The PodCertificateRequest gate is stable and on by default from 1.37.

DRA-EXAMPLE-DRIVER-SHARING-ENV​

dra-example-driver v0.5.0 (2026-09-16) is still the latest release. Its chart turns every sharing representation off by default: kubeletPlugin.gpuPartitions: 0 (partitionable devices off), gpuAllowMultipleAllocations: false (consumable-capacity sharing off) and gpuDeviceStatus: false (claim device-status data off). DCGM-EXPORTER-FAKE-GPUS​

dcgm-exporter's latest release is 4.9.0 (2026-11-06). What runs the exporter with no card is a mock NVML library underneath it: on NVIDIA's Mokka nvml-mock v0.7.0 (the t4 profile, on kind 1.37), the unmodified dcgm-exporter 4.5.3-4.8.2, the version Mokka's own CI runs, initialised DCGM against the mock and published DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS for injected Xid 48, 63 and 79 on arm64 under Rancher Desktop and on amd64 (SPIKE-09). The series is absent until an Xid is held, and DCGM keeps the last Xid per device.

NVIDIA-MIG-TIMESLICING-ISOLATION​

NVIDIA's GPU Operator documentation describes a MIG instance as a partition that provides memory and fault isolation at the hardware layer, and states that time-slicing has no memory or fault isolation between the replicas of one GPU. The device plugin's README says the same of time-slicing: each workload can reach the GPU's memory and runs in the same fault domain as the others, so if one crashes they all do.

Verified on2026-11-12

ConditionsNVIDIA/cloud-native-docs at d856ea69 gpu-operator/gpu-sharing.rst:25-26 (time-slicing: no memory or fault-isolation between replicas), :56-58 (MIG: memory and fault isolation at the hardware layer), :65-66; NVIDIA/k8s-device-plugin tag v0.20.1 (03296a61) README.md:367-371 (same fault-domain)
Stated insite/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lesson.md

NVIDIA-MPS-ERROR-CONTAINMENT​

With NVIDIA's Multi-Process Service, client processes have isolated GPU address spaces and the control daemon limits each client's memory and compute share, but error containment is limited: a fatal GPU fault from one client is reported to every client sharing that GPU, and the affected clients must exit.

Stated insite/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lesson.md

HAMI-CORE-ISOLATION​

HAMi-core is a library, libvgpu.so, preloaded into the workload's container. It intercepts the calls between the CUDA runtime and the CUDA driver (and the matching NVML calls) to hold a process to a per-device memory limit and an SM utilization limit, the latter by a time-sharing scheme of its own. Its enforcement therefore lives inside the workload's own process: it is a quota on cooperative CUDA use, not a hardware or kernel boundary, and it gives none of MIG's hardware memory or fault isolation. It is not vGPU.

FieldValue
Gradecited
Verified on2026-11-17
Sourcehttps://github.com/Project-HAMi/HAMi-core/blob/1fc9ea59cf1f94e16a7ddf6f445e129d282e1d03/README.md
ConditionsProject-HAMi/HAMi-core at 1fc9ea59 (main; the repository has no tags) README.md:8 (intercepts CUDA calls to enforce per-container device memory and compute limits), :23-24 (memory virtualization; SM utilization limited 'with a self-implemented time-sharing scheme'), :31 (hijacks the calls between libcudart.so and libcuda.so, and hooks NVML), :71-77 (LD_PRELOAD of libvgpu.so with CUDA_DEVICE_MEMORY_LIMIT and CUDA_DEVICE_SM_LIMIT); Project-HAMi/KAI-resource-isolator at 23ff3685 README.md:5 and :14, :19-20 (a webhook patches /etc/ld.so.preload so libvgpu.so loads at container start and caps CUDA memory allocation)
Stated insite/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lesson.md, site/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/optional-lab.md, site/docs/m5-ai-aware-scheduling-and-capacity/lesson.md
Re-check whenA HAMi-core README change to its mechanism section, a HAMi release that changes how HAMi-core is mounted, or a published HAMi fault-isolation benchmark read with its conditions. Re-read before M4 records.
How it was settledThe mechanism (a preloaded library intercepting CUDA calls) is read from both READMEs. "Not a hardware or kernel boundary" and "none of MIG's isolation" are inferences from where the enforcement lives, not a measured result: no CUDA ran in SPIKE-10 and HAMi-core was not even mounted there. So the entry is graded cited, and no page may say HAMi isolation was demonstrated (C9).

HAMI-CUDA-DISABLE-CONTROL-NO-PRELOAD​

At HAMi v2.11.0, the NVIDIA device plugin mounts /etc/ld.so.preload (the file that loads HAMi-core, libvgpu.so) into a container only when that container does not set CUDA_DISABLE_CONTROL=true. A container that sets it, as HAMi's Lab 5 instructs for a machine with no CUDA, gets no preload file, so HAMi-core is not loaded and the memory and core caps reach the container only as the environment variables CUDA_DEVICE_MEMORY_LIMIT_<n> and CUDA_DEVICE_SM_LIMIT, which nothing reads.

KAI-SHARING-DEFAULT-NONMEMORYENFORCED​

KAI Scheduler v0.18.8 documents three GPU-sharing modes. NonMemoryEnforced is the default: it schedules fractional GPU workloads without runtime memory isolation, and containers may see and use more GPU memory than requested. HamiCore enforces memory limits through HAMi-core. NvFractions uses NVIDIA's gpu-fractioning runtime path.

HAMI-DRA-CONSUMABLE-CAPACITY​

HAMi-DRA is HAMi's DRA path: a mutating webhook that turns nvidia.com/gpu-style requests into ResourceClaims, with a DRA driver that slices device memory and cores through DRA consumable capacity, which it requires. NVIDIA-XID-48-63-79​

In NVIDIA's XID catalogue, XID 48 is a double-bit ECC error the GPU could not correct, XID 63 is a memory row-remapping event (dynamic page retirement on earlier GPUs), and XID 79 means the GPU has fallen off the bus: the driver found it not accessible over PCI Express. A row remap takes effect only after a GPU reset.

Gradecited

Stated insite/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lesson.md, site/docs/m4-gpu-enabled-nodes/lab.md
Re-check whenA change to NVIDIA's XID catalogue for these three codes. Re-read before M4 and M12 record.
How it was settledThe catalogue is a published page, not a pinned artifact, so the entry is cited. The lab's fixture m4/xid-events.txt is course-authored; this entry sources only the meanings its caption says it paraphrases.

dcgmi diag -r takes four run levels: 1 is a quick system validation of seconds, 2 an extended system validation of about two minutes, 3 hardware diagnostics of about fifteen minutes, and 4 longer-running hardware diagnostics.

KATA-VM-PER-POD-KVM​

Kata Containers implements the Kubernetes sandbox at the Pod level with a lightweight virtual machine that runs its own guest Linux kernel, with the hypervisor as the isolation boundary. Every hypervisor it supports (QEMU, Cloud Hypervisor, Firecracker, Dragonball) uses KVM as the hardware virtualisation interface, so the node must expose hardware virtualisation.

GVISOR-SENTRY-GOFER​

gVisor's Sentry is a kernel-like component written in Go that handles the sandboxed workload's system calls itself and may make only limited host system calls, under a system call filter. Requests it cannot serve from that restricted environment, file access among them, go to a separate, slightly more privileged Gofer process. containerd starts it through the io.containerd.runsc.v1 shim.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-11-12

Stated insite/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/lesson.md, site/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/deep-dive.md

MCP-SPEC-2025-03-26-TOOLS​

AUDIT-REFUSED-CREATE-NO-NAME​

An audit event for a create that authorization refuses carries no objectRef.name. The event's object reference is built from the request's URL, and the name of a created object is filled in only when the handler decodes the request body, which a refused request never reaches. A refused patch keeps its name, because the name is in the URL.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-11-12
Sourcehttps://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/v1.37.0/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/request.go
Conditionskubernetes/kubernetes tag v1.37.0 staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/request.go:70-78 (ObjectRef from the request attributes, Name: attribs.GetName()) and :125-135 (LogRequestObject fills an empty name from the decoded object); measured: planning/lab-tests/05-grid-sandbox-audit.md:135 and :248 (finding 1); live: planning/lab-tests/raw/m11/lab/43-audit-read.txt
Stated insite/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/lab.md
Re-check whenA Kubernetes minor release that changes apiserver/pkg/audit/request.go. Re-measure with the lab's audit read.

RUNTIMECLASS-HANDLER-NO-FALLBACK​

A Pod naming a RuntimeClass that does not exist is refused at admission ("pod rejected: RuntimeClass ... not found"). A Pod whose RuntimeClass names a handler the node's containerd does not configure is scheduled but never starts: sandbox creation fails with no runtime for "<handler>" is configured, the kubelet records FailedCreatePodSandBox and retries, and the Pod stays in ContainerCreating. Nothing falls back to the default runtime; only a Pod with no runtimeClassName (nil or empty) resolves to the empty handler, which containerd maps to its default runtime.

FieldValue
Gradeverified
Verified on2026-11-13
Sourcehttps://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/v1.37.0/pkg/kubelet/runtimeclass/runtimeclass_manager.go
Conditionskubernetes/kubernetes tag v1.37.0 pkg/kubelet/runtimeclass/runtimeclass_manager.go:61-78 (nil or empty name resolves to the empty handler; otherwise the RuntimeClass's handler or an error), pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_sandbox.go:59-75 (the handler is passed to RunPodSandbox; an error returns, no retry with another handler), pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go:1732-1753 (FailedCreatePodSandBox event) and plugin/pkg/admission/runtimeclass/admission.go:155-158 (Forbidden, RuntimeClass not found); containerd/containerd tag v2.3.4 (the node image's containerd) internal/cri/config/config.go:731-759 (only an empty handler becomes DefaultRuntimeName; an unknown one returns no runtime for %q is configured). Observed live on grid-sandbox 2026-11-13: planning/lab-tests/raw/m11/handler-probe/h03-handler-missing.txt (Pending, ContainerCreating, three FailedCreatePodSandBox events in 32 s), h04-node-logs.txt (kubelet and containerd journals; crictl lists no sandbox) and h05-runtimeclass-object-missing.txt (Forbidden at create); source excerpts in source-reads.txt
Stated insite/docs/m11-agents-as-a-workload-class/lesson.md
Re-check whenA Kubernetes minor that changes kubelet RuntimeClass resolution or the RuntimeClass admission plugin, or a containerd minor in the kind node image that changes GetSandboxRuntime. Re-read with the Kubernetes pin before M11 records.

K137-PODGROUP-EXTENSION-POINTS​

Kubernetes 1.37's scheduler framework has two pod-group extension points: PlacementFeasible, called after each member of a pod group is evaluated, to decide whether the group is schedulable, may become schedulable, or will not become schedulable in the current placement; and PodGroupPostFilter, called after a PodGroup cannot be scheduled.

LANDSCAPE-SCHEDULER-PLUGINS​

scheduler-plugins is a Kubernetes SIG Scheduling subproject. Its latest release, v0.35.7 (2026-09-08), is built against Kubernetes v1.35.7. It ships a kube-scheduler binary with out-of-tree plugins, among them Coscheduling (a PodGroup with minMember), Capacity Scheduling (ElasticQuota), Node Resource Topology, Network-Aware Scheduling and Preemption Toleration, at scheduling.x-k8s.io/v1alpha1. It installs either as a second scheduler or as the replacement for the default one, and its own install guide says running more than one scheduler "will inevitably encounter resource conflicts when the cluster is short of resources".

LANDSCAPE-MULTIKUEUE​

MultiKueue is Kueue's multi-cluster dispatching. At Kueue v0.20.6 it is beta and enabled by default (beta since v0.9), and a MultiKueue setup is a manager cluster plus at least one worker cluster. Its MultiKueueCluster and MultiKueueConfig objects are served at kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2. Because it needs two clusters, the course teaches it as a brief.
